STATUS: IN PROGRESS

# Progress

Tick a task only after its tests pass and the change is committed. Tasks run in
order inside a phase. A later phase may start only when the gate of the phase
before it passes, except where a task says otherwise.

- [x] T0.3b Spike runner behind `npm run spike`: generate files if missing, serve a production build, one fresh page per file, renderer memory sampled from Node (max RSS), tab-crash and timeout handling, raw results as JSON, files deleted in a `finally`. Exit 0 even when a run crashes.
- [x] T0.4a Streaming xlsx reader in `src/engine/xlsx-stream/`: zip directory, `DecompressionStream`, shared strings, styles (dates), first sheet, rows as an async generator. Unit tests compare it with SheetJS on small files.

- [x] T0.5 Implement the size guard: when a file is above the measured safe size, show the "save as csv" warning instead of loading.

## Phase 6: Canvas drag, input regions, parameters in input boxes, Reference table step (gates G1 to G6)

Read SPEC section 13 and the Phase 6 parts of `docs/DESIGN.md` first. Order: the drag (no server), parameters in the input boxes and the
Reference table step (engine, then the migration, then the screens), the input region (fixtures first, then the engine, the
analysis, the screens), then the quality pass, docs and the final run. The only new migration is `20261118000018` (T6.9). Every task starts with the
failing test. A task that changes a screen also updates its routes in `ui-rules/routes.ts` in the same commit and runs the
UI rules for those routes in light at 1280 and 1440 px and in dark at 1440 px; the full `npm run test:ui-rules` runs in
T6.22 and T6.23. No fixture in `testcase/` is read or changed; the layout files of this phase are made by a script.

Dragging steps (SPEC 13.1)
- [x] T6.1 Measure and find the cause. A script `npm run perf:canvas` (not a gate) opens the builder with the 12-step timesheet flow and with a generated 60-step flow, drags a card with `page.mouse` in 60 moves, and records the time between frames (`requestAnimationFrame`), how many times `BuilderScreen`, `SettingsPanel`, `StepList` and each `StepCard` render during the drag (a counter behind a dev flag that is off in the release build), whether every card stays in the page and visible, and a screenshot at the middle of the drag. It writes the numbers to `docs/CANVAS_PERF.md` (a table, regenerated by the script). Record the cause in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. The task changes no behaviour.
- [x] T6.2 Fix it, test first: `e2e/canvas-drag.spec.ts` asserts the budgets of SPEC 13.1 (95th percentile of the frame time at most 33 ms with 12 steps and 50 ms with 60), that no card disappears during the drag and after the drop, that the edges follow, that one drag makes one change of the definition (the "unsaved changes" state and the render counters), that the selection is kept, and that a read-only version cannot be dragged. Typical fixes, to be chosen from T6.1: keep the position of a held card in React Flow's own state and write it to the definition on `onNodeDragStop`; memoize `StepCard` and keep the callbacks and `nodeTypes` stable; compute `nodes` and `edges` without the selection and the text of the whole definition on every move; remove a transition or a filter that runs on every frame. Record the numbers that were reached in `docs/CANVAS_PERF.md` and keep a margin of at least 20% under the budget in the test.

## Phase 7: Harold design system (gates G1 to G6)

Read SPEC section 14, the section "Harold design system (Phase 7)" of `docs/DESIGN.md` and the snapshot in `design/harold/` first
(`README.md`, `tokens.json`, then the README of each component you restyle, and `bundle.css` and `bundle.js` for exact values). This phase
restyles the app and changes no behaviour: `src/engine/`, `src/store/`, `supabase/` and `flows/` are not edited (check with
`git diff main -- src/engine src/store supabase flows` in every task that ends with a commit), no migration, no new catalog wording, and
`testcase/` is not touched. Order: tokens, fonts, scene and themes, glass, icons; then the components; then the shell; then the screens;
then the builder; then the rules sweep, the docs and the review.

How to keep every commit green while the look changes: the old token names stay as aliases of Harold tokens until T7.17, so a screen that
is not moved yet still renders. A rule of the UI rules changes in the same commit as the change it checks; where one change spans two tasks
the rule accepts both values until the second task, which removes the old one. Every task starts with the failing test. A task that changes a
screen runs the UI rules for the routes it touched (`npx playwright test -c playwright.ui-rules.config.ts -g "<route text>"`) in light at
1280 and 1440 px and in dark at 1440 px, and the e2e specs of that screen. The full `npm run test:ui-rules` takes about 55 minutes: run it in
the background with a timeout of 7,200,000 ms, redirect its output to a file and print `EXIT $?`; it runs in T7.17, T7.19 and T7.20. The drag
budgets of `e2e/canvas-drag.spec.ts` are never loosened: a restyle that breaks them is changed, not the budget. No fixture in `testcase/` is read or changed.

Foundation
- [x] T7.1 The Harold tokens in `src/ui/tokens.css` for both themes under `:root[data-theme]`, beside the old names (no look change yet): every token of `design/harold/tokens.json` with the same name and value (colour, spacing, radius, shadow, size, blur; an alias value such as `{accent}` becomes a `var()`), and the type styles as variables. Tests first in `src/ui/tokens.test.ts`: parity with the snapshot (the test reads `design/harold/tokens.json` and fails when a name or a value differs, or when a token is missing); the contrast of every text and ground pair of both themes with each translucent surface composited over `scene-100` and over `scene-200` (4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for `border-strong`, the focus ring and meaningful icons); an inventory test that lists every CSS variable still used by the CSS modules and says which are old names (the list shrinks to empty by T7.17). A pair that fails its own target is written to Blockers and to "Needs Harold" with the numbers; the value is not changed.
- [x] T7.2 Fonts and type. Add `@fontsource/be-vietnam-pro` (400, 500, 600, 700; the `latin` and `vietnamese` subsets) and `@fontsource/jetbrains-mono` (400, 500; `latin`), pinned, and remove `@fontsource/plus-jakarta-sans`; if the packages cannot be installed, write the host under Blockers and stop (no CDN, no Google Fonts). `src/ui/type.css` holds the styles of DESIGN "Type" as classes or variables; `--font-sans` and `--font-mono` point to the new families. Tests: a unit test that every style of the table exists with its size, line height and weight; an e2e that `document.fonts.check` is true for both families after load, that a Vietnamese line with stacked marks (a sample built in the test, not a catalog text) is not clipped in a single-line element, and that no request goes to a font host (extend `acceptance/privacy.spec.ts` and run it); `npm run build:pages` stays under its size limit. ## Phase 8: Faster building and more steps (gates G1 to G6)

Read SPEC section 15 and the Phase 8 parts of `docs/DESIGN.md` first. No server part: no migration, no change to `src/store/` or `supabase/`.
Order: the canvas (arrange, then adding steps), the preview popup, then the definition format and the seven steps (each: engine with
unit tests, then the form), the step list, and the quality pass. Every task starts with the failing test. A task that changes a screen also updates
its routes in `ui-rules/routes.ts` in the same commit and runs the UI rules for those routes in light at 1280 and 1440 px and in dark at
1440 px; the full `npm run test:ui-rules` runs in T8.19 and T8.20. `testcase/` is not read or changed, `flows/monthly-timesheet.flow.json`
is not edited, `npm run test:e2e` keeps the canvas drag spec unchanged, and every new text is a catalog key in English and Vietnamese.

Canvas (SPEC 15.1, 15.2)
- [x] T8.1 The arrange layout as pure functions in `src/engine/flow/arrange.ts` (no React): `arrangeLayout(definition, options)` (layers by the longest path, order inside a layer by the average row of the neighbours, `match` above `nomatch`, gaps of 80 and 40 px, layers centred on one axis, flows that are not connected stacked 120 px apart, a selection keeps the top left of its box, the same result when run twice), `alignNodes`, `distributeNodes`. Unit tests for each rule, an empty flow, one step, a diamond, two Filter outputs that join again, a flow of 60 steps in under 50 ms.
- [x] T8.2 Auto arrange on the canvas: the control button, the shortcut, the whole flow or the selection, one change of the definition ("unsaved changes" once), fit view, the toast with Undo (puts every position back, goes with the next change), disabled on a published version. e2e (a messy flow becomes ordered, Undo, selection only) and the routes of the UI rules. `e2e/canvas-drag.spec.ts` is not touched and still passes.
- [x] T8.3 Align and distribute and Snap to grid: the selection toolbar (`role="toolbar"`), the six align and two distribute actions as one change each, the Snap switch kept per user in the browser. e2e for each action and a keyboard path; the routes of the UI rules.

## Phase 9: Print layout and linked reference tables (gates G1 to G6)

Read SPEC section 16 and the Phase 9 parts of `docs/DESIGN.md` first. Phase 8 must be complete and merged (the wide dialog and the schema version 4 come from it). The only new migration
is `20261118000021` (T9.12). The only new dependency is ExcelJS (T9.1). Order: measure ExcelJS, the print settings, the writer, the integration and the form, the
print preview; then the links (pure rules, the stores, the migration, the screens); then the quality pass. Every task starts with the failing test; a task that changes a screen
updates its routes in `ui-rules/routes.ts` and runs the UI rules for them in both themes; the full `npm run test:ui-rules` runs in T9.19 and T9.20. `testcase/` is not read or
changed and the acceptance flow is not edited.

Print layout (SPEC 16.1)
- [ ] T9.1 Measure and decide: add ExcelJS (pin the version, record why in `docs/DECISIONS.md`), loaded only through a dynamic import so it is a separate file of the build and the main file does not grow (a test checks the main file size and that the file is not requested when no layout is on). A script `npm run perf:print` (not a gate) writes 5,000, 20,000 and 50,000 rows by 12 columns with a print layout in headless Chromium and records the time, the memory and the file size in `docs/PRINT_PERF.md`; the row limit of SPEC 16.1 item 7 is 20,000 unless the measure says less (then SPEC and DECISIONS are changed in this commit). `npm run build:pages` stays under its file limit.
- [ ] T9.2 The print settings: types for `settings.print` in the Output node, the JSON schema, the validation with clear messages (paper, orientation, margins, at most 6 header lines of at most 200 characters, 1 to 5 signers, unknown tokens and unknown parameters by name), `schemaVersion` 5 for a definition that has one, and the token replacement as a pure function (`{effective_date}`, `{month_year}`, `{run_date}`, `{flow}`, `{rows}`, `{param:key}`, `{day}`, `{month}`, `{year}`) with unit tests. Older definitions load unchanged.
- [ ] T9.3 The writer, part 1 (`src/engine/output/print/`, no React): `buildPrintWorkbook` with ExcelJS: the page (paper, orientation, margins, fit to one page wide, the repeated header row, no gridlines, print area), the table (borders, bold header on a light fill, number and date formats, widths from the content, wrapped long text). Tests read the file back with SheetJS and compare every cell with the same table written by the normal path, and read the page setup from the file's XML.
- [ ] T9.4 The writer, part 2: the header lines (merged across the table, alignment, bold, sizes, tokens), the signature block (the date line, 1 to 5 signers in equal columns, the space to sign, the optional name), the page-break rule so the block is not cut (the estimate of rows per page from paper, orientation and row height), the footer "Trang n/N" in both languages. Tests on the XML (merges, breaks, footer) and on the readback.
- [ ] T9.5 Into the output: `buildOutputFiles` uses the writer for a workbook with at least one print layout (each sheet its own layout, sheets without one are plain), refuses a table over the row limit with the message, ignores the group for csv, and the normal path is byte-for-byte the same as before for a flow with no layout (a test compares the bytes of the acceptance flow's workbook with the stored result of the normal path). `acceptance/print.spec.ts` (part of `npm run test:acceptance`) runs a generated flow with a layout through the real engine in Chromium and checks the table, the page setup, the lines and the signatures in the file; both acceptance datasets still pass.
- [ ] T9.6 The Output form, Configure tab: the group "Print layout" of DESIGN (the switch, the page controls, the header lines, the signatures, the defaults, the errors), with the parameter control where the registry allows. e2e for each control and for each error; routes of the UI rules in both themes with the pseudo-locale check.
- [ ] T9.7 The print preview on the Test tab: the two page boxes (first and last page), the "approximate" note, "Open large" in the wide dialog, the state with no rows. Unit tests of the page-drawing function, e2e, routes of the UI rules.

## Needs Harold

Decisions with a default. Build with the default unless this list says otherwise.

| Topic | Default used by the build |
| --- | --- |
| Phase 7: the one `primary` button of a sidebar screen | "Create flow" in the sidebar, as DESIGN says; the page's own main action (Run, Download, New table) is `secondary`. If Harold wants the page action to be the primary one, the sidebar button becomes `secondary` instead (D99) |
| Phase 7: the `danger` fill in the dark theme | Harold's token as it is (a pale salmon `#ffb0a9` with dark text): readable, but it stands out on a dark screen (UI_AUDIT_P7 finding 8). Harold decides whether to soften it |
| OT type of a day | From the attendance code of the same employee on the same day (rule R9) |
| Week for the weekly OT limit | ISO week, only days of the run month (rule R11) |
| Hours equal to a limit | Not a breach (rule R11) |
| Formula language | Tier 1 only |
| Approval before a version takes effect | None |
| Reference tables by non-admin users | Not allowed |
| UI language | English (Phase 4: English default plus Vietnamese, see below) |
| Where the two DuckDB-WASM files are served (above the 25 MiB per-file limit of Cloudflare Pages) | Decided by Harold on 2026-11-18: from jsDelivr in the Pages build (`npm run build:pages`, D71). Other builds still serve them (D7) |
| Phase 4: default theme | System (follows the operating system) |
| Phase 4: Vietnamese texts | The build writes a first Vietnamese translation of every key (T4.7e). Harold reviews the wording on the Texts tab after the phase; the build does not wait for it |
| Phase 4: who may edit texts | Active admins only |
| Phase 4: texts before sign-in | Readable without signing in (they hold no personal data), so the sign-in screen can show edited texts |
| Phase 4: password rule | At least 10 characters; Generate makes 16 characters. No other complexity rule |
| Phase 4: effect of Lock | Immediate: the open session loses all access through row-level security, and Auth refuses new sign-ins |
| Phase 4: delete a user | Not offered. Lock instead (the user's flows and run history stay) |
| Phase 4: parameter in formulas | `PARAM("key")`; in the SQL step a one-row table `params` (D78) |
| Phase 4: remember parameter values between runs | No (values may be personal data); the defaults of the definition fill the form |
| Phase 5: who sees which reference table | The admin sees all. Every user sees the name and the description of every table (so Lookup and Pivot can offer it) and opens the rows of a table only when a flow they own, or a shared flow of their groups, uses it (SPEC 12.1). If Harold wants every user to read every table as before, drop T5.17 and T5.18 and keep the rest |
| Phase 5: reference table import | `.xlsx` and `.csv`, up to 50,000 rows and 100 columns, always a draft, never published by the import. The name of a table cannot change (flows point to it by name) |
| Phase 5: idle sign-out limit | Default 8 hours, an admin sets 1 to 72, a warning 2 minutes before, activity includes a run in progress, checked in the browser (the token lives up to one hour more) |
| Phase 5: unsaved work at sign-out | Lost; the warning dialog says so. There is no autosave |
| Phase 5: Texts tab | One dialog with both languages and one atomic Save; no language to pick. A text equal to its default is not stored |
| Phase 5: collapsible panels | The sidebar collapses to a 64 px rail, the other three panels to 48 px rails; the state is kept per user in the browser; a click on a step card opens a collapsed settings panel |
| Phase 6: sample file for the analysis | The file is read in the browser only and never stored; only the settings that the analysis proposes are saved with the flow. The analysis proposes, the person applies (SPEC 13.2). Anyone who can edit the flow can use it, on the Test tab of the Input step |
| Phase 6: layout fixtures | No real file was given. The build makes its own layouts with a script (title block, table, footer, merged cells, two tables, a later header row, in Vietnamese and English). If Harold later gives redacted real files, they are added in a later phase after he agrees to the layout and the words |
| Phase 6: one table per Input step | The Input step reads one table. A sheet with two tables is read by two Input steps with different regions; the analysis lists the other tables. A header of two or more rows is out of scope |
| Phase 6: stop rule | End of the sheet (as today), the first empty row (1 to 10 in a row), a row with a text (contains, starts with, equals; not case sensitive; the row is not read), or a fixed row. If the marker text is never found the whole table to the end is read and a warning says so |
| Phase 6: required columns | Optional. When set, the file must contain every listed column (case and order do not matter, extra columns are allowed); otherwise the run stops before it starts and names the missing columns |
| Phase 6: parameters in input boxes | Every single-value setting of every step (text, number, date, yes/no, fixed-choice dropdown) can take a parameter; column pickers, list and grid rows, formulas and SQL cannot (they use `PARAM` and `params`). The Parameters panel on the right does not change |
| Phase 6: Reference table step | One entry in the step list; the table is chosen after the step is on the canvas. It uses the version of the table valid on the effective date of the run (the date that selects the flow version), with no pinned version. A date check warns, and does not block, when a table has no published version on some day of a flow version's range |
| Phase 6: who reads a table that only the Reference table step uses | The same rule as Lookup and Pivot (SPEC 12.1): a person who may run the flow reads the table's rows. Migration 18 makes the database say so |
| Phase 6: drag budget | 95th percentile of the frame time at most 33 ms with 12 steps and 50 ms with 60 steps in headless Chromium, no card disappears, one drag is one change. T6.2 records what was reached and keeps a 20% margin |
| Phase 7: where the look comes from | The Harold design system (Spatial), snapshot in `design/harold/`. It replaces the teal look in both themes; there is no way to switch back. The seven screens in `design/screens/` stay as the reference of structure and layout only |
| Phase 7: default theme | System stays the default. When the browser reports no preference the app is dark (Harold's default) |
| Phase 7: the orange call-to-action | Removed. The one main action of a screen is the one `primary` blue button; the buttons that were teal `primary` become `secondary`. Ember (`accent`) is used at most once per screen, and the build uses it for the Draft state of a version only |
| Phase 7: text size | The Harold scale as written: `body` 16 px, `label` 14 px for labels, buttons, tabs and table headers, `small` 14 px for table cells and helper text. If the 380 px settings panel or a 1280 px layout does not fit at 16 px, the build uses `small` there and records it; it does not shrink Harold's sizes |
| Phase 7: how much glass | No limit. Harold's glass recipe may be used on any surface, in any number, tables, grids, step cards and canvas controls included; floating layers (dialog, menu, dropdown, date picker, popover, toast, sticky header) use `surface-overlay`. The only safeguard is the drag budget (SPEC 13.1): if blur on an element makes it fail, the build removes the blur from that element, records it, and keeps the budget |
| Phase 7: dates and numbers | No change: `30 Sep 2026` and the present number format in both languages. Harold's Vietnamese formats (`20/11/2026`, `3.024`) need a locale-aware formatter and are a later phase |
| Phase 7: parts of Harold for phones and charts | Not used (app bar, bottom navigation, sheet, list item, KPI tile, avatar, the chart colours). The app is for laptops, 1280 px and wider |
| Phase 7: fonts | Be Vietnam Pro and JetBrains Mono, served by the app through `@fontsource` (no request to Google Fonts: the privacy test forbids it). JetBrains Mono is new, for formulas, keys and SQL |
| Phase 7: the width of a collapsing panel | Keeps its 200 ms animation (Phase 5); the panel has no blur while it moves, so Harold's rule "never animate a blurred element's size" holds |
| Phase 7: logo | None, as in Harold: the app name in `sans` 700 next to a `radius-sm` blue square |
| Phase 7: a Harold value that fails its own contrast target | The build records the pair and the numbers under Blockers and here, keeps Harold's value and uses the pair only where it passes; Harold decides whether to change the system |
| Phase 8: Auto arrange | Left to right in layers (the longest path), 80 px between columns and 40 px between steps, the layers centred on one axis, unconnected flows stacked 120 px apart, the whole flow or the selected steps (two or more). One level of Undo in the toast; there is no general undo and redo (a candidate for a later phase) |
| Phase 8: adding a step after the selected one | Only when exactly one step is selected and it has a free output; the new step goes to the right and is connected to its first free required input; a Filter connects through `match`; Input file and Reference table never connect. A drop on an edge splits it |
| Phase 8: copy and paste | The clipboard is in memory (gone on reload, never sent). Input file keys are made unique. Edges between the copied steps are kept |
| Phase 8: preview popup | 100 rows as now; "Show more" runs again with up to 1,000 rows; Find works on the loaded rows; no sorting and no column resizing |
| Phase 8: search in the step list, undo and redo, date-range, ranking and running-total steps | Not built; candidates for a later phase. The step list has 19 entries in 8 groups |
| Phase 8: Sort and row order | Row order is kept by row-by-row steps and not promised after Group by, Pivot, Unpivot and Append. Empty values sort last |
| Phase 8: Remove duplicates, Top N | Empty equals empty; "keep first" is by the current row order; Top N keeps no ties and N is at most 1,000,000 |
| Phase 8: Split column and Clean text | Cut at the last space is the Vietnamese name mode. Marks are removed with the Unicode decomposition plus `đ` to `d`; precomposed and combining forms give the same result |
| Phase 8: Check | A rule is a condition that is true when the row is fine; a row that fails two rules is two rows in `errors`; the run goes on unless "Stop the run when a rule fails" is on |
| Phase 8: Compare | Keys must be unique in both inputs; values are compared typed; the columns that exist on one side only are not compared |
| Phase 9: print layout format | xlsx through ExcelJS, only for a sheet with the layout on, loaded as a separate file; no PDF; a workbook with no layout is written as before |
| Phase 9: header lines and tokens | Up to 6 lines; tokens `{effective_date}` (`30/09/2026`), `{month_year}` (`09/2026`), `{run_date}`, `{flow}`, `{rows}`, `{param:key}`, `{day}`, `{month}`, `{year}`; dates in the printed form `dd/mm/yyyy` whatever the language of the app |
| Phase 9: signature block | At the end of the report after the last row, 1 to 5 signers, a date line on the right; never cut by a page break (an estimate of the rows of a page decides) |
| Phase 9: page | A4, portrait, normal margins, fit to one page wide, header row repeated, page numbers "Trang n/N"; A3 and Letter are offered; no company logo |
| Phase 9: print row limit | 20,000 rows, or less if T9.1 measures less |
| Phase 9: linked tables: which values | Distinct, trimmed, blanks out, case and accents count; from the version of A that is published (the one in effect today when the link is made) |
| Phase 9: linked tables: when B changes | When a version of A is published; the new values go into a draft of B (one is made when there is none) and B is not published; a value that leaves A stays in B |
| Phase 9: the notice to the admin | In the app only (the table, a dot in the sidebar, "New" rows); no email; it closes when the draft with the values is published |
| Phase 9: chains and cycles | A feeds B and B feeds C is allowed; a cycle is refused with the names; a table has one source |

None. The Pages file size blocker of T3.9 is solved by D71.

### 2026-11-20 T6.17 The analysis of a sample file (turn 24)
Changed: src/engine/layout/ (new: analyze.ts, footer-words.ts, index.ts; no React, no Supabase, nothing leaves the browser): `analyzeLayout(grid, options)` finds the first table (a header row of 2 or more text cells with rows of data under it, which end at the first row that is not table-like) and proposes the header row, the first and last column, where to stop (a footer word under the table with the column it sits in, an empty row with text after it, text that is not a table, or the end of the sheet), the columns the file must have, the columns that look like merged cells, and the total rows to ignore, each with a confidence (high, medium, low) and a reason (a message id with parameters, `engine.layout*` in the engine, `en` and `vi`); it lists what is left out (the title rows above the header, the footer rows under the table) and the other tables found, reads at most 2,000 rows and 100 columns of the grid that `readGrid` gives (xlsx and csv), is deterministic, and says when the sheet is longer or wider. `proposalToSettings` turns a proposal into the settings of an Input step (defaults left out). `chooseSheet` takes the first sheet when it holds a table, else the sheet with the largest one. The footer words are generic words in English and Vietnamese in one module (`Prepared by`, `Signature`, `Người lập`, `Ký tên`, ...) and the total words (`Total`, `Subtotal`, `Tổng`).
Tests (24, src/engine/layout/analyze.test.ts): every clear layout, as xlsx and csv, gives its truth: header row, first and last column and the stop rule with high confidence and a reason, the column names, what is left out, fill down and the total rows; and the proposal applied to an Input step reads the rows of the truth (the first column and the amounts of every row); two tables give low confidence on the header, the columns and the stop rule and list the other table with its place; a table that nothing ends gives a low-confidence stop that says so; a sheet of text only gives no table and no settings; `chooseSheet` cases; the same answer twice; 2,000 rows in under 2 seconds; the 2,000 by 100 limit; and a scan of `src/` finds none of the generator's values.
Changed in the generator (T6.14) because the analysis showed that two layouts could not be told apart: a table followed by a blank row and then nothing looks like a table with no end, so the later-row layout now has a line under its blank row, and the "title" ranges of the truths are the rows from the first text to the last text above the header.
Found on the way: a row read from xlsx has holes where cells are missing, so `.map` skips them; the header is read by index. A first name `layout.*` for the messages failed the catalog test of known prefixes; they are `engine.layout*`.
Gates run: check PASS (105 files, 1421 tests, exit 0, read before this commit); test:acceptance PASS (22 passed, with the changed generator).

The csv path now decides the delimiter and the width itself, reads with an explicit dialect and column list, and numbers the rows by the line of the file; the xlsx reader counts a gap in the row numbers as empty rows. Every csv now goes through this path, so the acceptance gate was run.

How the test works: the step modules have no common parser to call, so the test reads the source of each module (the part that parses the settings for the Input step) and collects every name read as `raw.x`, `settings.x`, `raw['x']`, `int('x')` or `text('x')`. That list must equal the registry plus the exclusions for each of the 11 step types, in both directions (a new setting fails the test; a stale entry fails it too). This is a source scan, not a run of a parser with defaults as the task text says; the reason is in this log. The settings that T6.15 adds to the Input step are added in T6.15.
Choices made: the Input step's `key` is not bindable (the Run screen needs the key to show one file slot before any parameter has a value); the Output `columns`, `numberLikeText` and `sort`, and every list, grid, column picker, formula and SQL are excluded.

### 2026-11-20 T6.1 Measure the canvas drag and find the cause (turn 1)
Changed: `npm run perf:canvas` (scripts/perf-canvas.ts, scripts/lib/canvas-perf.ts with tests), render counters behind `VITE_PERF_COUNTERS` (src/ui/builder/render-counter.ts with tests; called in BuilderScreen, SettingsPanel, StepList, StepCard), docs/CANVAS_PERF.md and docs/canvas-perf/ screenshots, D87 in docs/DECISIONS.md, `dist-perf/` in .gitignore. No behaviour change.
Cause: every card is `visibility: hidden` during a drag (the controlled `nodes` carry no `measured` size, so React Flow hides them until it measures again), and each pointer move renders BuilderScreen, SettingsPanel and StepList 61 times and all cards 61 times each. Frame times in headless Chromium are 17 ms at p95 even so. Note: the timesheet flow has 70 steps, not 12; the report measures it plus generated flows of 12 and 60 steps.
Gates run: check PASS (89 files, 1144 tests, exit 0); perf:canvas exit 0 (not a gate). The release build was not inspected for the counters; the flag is a build-time constant.
Next: T6.2 (fix, test first).

### 2026-11-18 T4.8b Edge Function admin-users and gate G6 (turn 23)
Changed: supabase/functions/admin-users/index.ts (create, reset_password, lock, unlock; fetch only), supabase/migrations/20261118000012_end_sessions.sql (`admin_end_sessions`), supabase/config.toml (Edge Runtime on, function entry), scripts/test-functions.mjs and vitest.functions.config.ts, integration-functions/admin-users.test.ts (14 tests), package.json (`test:functions`, and `npm run gates` now ends with it), scripts/test-db.mjs (skips the Edge Runtime like the other services), 120-account-lock.test.sql (the definer list has the new function). D76 records the design.
Finding: the first version imported `@supabase/supabase-js` with an `npm:` specifier; the Edge Runtime in the container failed with `invalid peer certificate: UnknownIssuer` for registry.npmjs.org (the network of this environment re-signs TLS). I did not look for a mirror: the function now has no dependency and needs no download.
Verified on the local stack (not assumed): an Auth ban alone does not end an open session, so `admin_end_sessions` is used; after a lock and after a reset the old refresh token is refused; a token issued before the lock still reaches the gateway and is refused by the database and by the function.
Cases covered by `npm run test:functions`: no token, anon key, bad token (401); a user for every action (403); a locked admin with a valid token (403) and after the unlock (200); locking through the function ends the sessions; bad JSON, an array, an unknown action, GET (400); create (sign-in works, flag set, role admin, no flag); input errors (email, short password, role, existing email); reset (old password refused, new works, old refresh token refused, flag set); lock (open session reads and writes nothing, cannot sign in or refresh, unlock restores); self-lock (409); unknown or malformed id; the last active admin is protected in the database also for the service role.
Gates run: check PASS (881 tests, exit 0); `npm run test:db` exit 0 (13 files, 349 tests); `npm run test:functions` exit 0 on a local Supabase with the Edge Runtime (1 file, 14 tests).
Next: T4.8c (AccountAdmin service).

### 2026-11-18 T4.3 React Flow follows the app theme (turn 3)
Changed: src/ui/builder/canvas.tsx, builder.module.css (React Flow variables mapped to tokens), e2e/canvas-theme.spec.ts (2 tests: OS dark with app Light gives a light canvas, OS light with app Dark gives a dark canvas; background, dots, edge colours and colour-scheme checked).
Finding: the installed @xyflow/react 13.0.1 has no `colorMode` prop (the task text and the advisor assumed v12 behaviour). Its stylesheet uses `light-dark()` with `color-scheme: light dark`, and `.light .react-flow` or `.dark .react-flow` classes on an ancestor override it. So the canvas wrapper gets the class `light` or `dark` from the app theme.
Test order: the first run of the new test failed for a wrong reason (the seed flow has no edge), so it was rewritten to build one; with the old canvas code unchanged the colour-scheme assertion was not run separately, so "failing first" is only shown by the dots colour (`rgb(145,145,154)` instead of the token).
Gates run: check PASS (68 files, 723 tests, exit 0); builder, settings-panel and canvas-theme e2e PASS (builder + canvas-theme re-run after the class change: 11 passed). Full e2e and ui-rules not run (T4.4 covers ui-rules).

### 2026-11-17 T2.4 Checkbox, Tabs, Dialog, Toast, Skeleton, Empty state, Error state
Changed: src/ui/components/{checkbox,tabs,tab-keys,dialog,toast,toast-state,skeleton,states}.ts(x) with CSS modules, alert/inbox/close icons, more.test.tsx (12 tests).
How: Checkbox is a drawn box on a `role=checkbox` button (no input). Tabs: `role=tablist/tab/tabpanel`, roving tabindex, arrows wrap, Home/End, 40 px tabs with a 2 px accent underline; Chip and ChipGroup (36 px, `aria-pressed`). Dialog: modal, focus moves in, Tab wraps (`trapIndex`), Escape calls `onClose`, focus returns to the opener on unmount. Toast: provider with a polite live region, 3 kept, 5 s, dismiss button. Skeleton: `SkeletonTable` with the real heights (header 41, row 57), `aria-busy`, bars `aria-hidden`, pulse that stops with reduced motion; `useDelayedLoading` shows it only after 300 ms. ### 2026-11-17 T0.4a Streaming xlsx reader
Changed: src/engine/xlsx-stream/{errors,zip,parts,sheet,index}.ts and 12 tests (SheetJS as oracle on small workbooks and on a generated 2,001-row benchmark file; chunk sizes down to 1 character; special characters; dates; gaps; unknown sheet; not a zip). PROGRESS: T0.4 split into T0.4a and T0.4b.
How: reads the zip directory with small slices, inflates one part at a time with the browser's `DecompressionStream`, parses the sheet XML in chunks row by row, resolves shared strings and date styles. No new dependency.
Check: three deliberate mutations (no XML unescape, date styles ignored, no tail kept between chunks) each made tests fail, then were reverted. Node sanity run on the 1,048,576 x 40 xlsx that SheetJS could not open: all 1,048,576 rows read in 51.5 s, process RSS 384 MB, last row id 1048575. Browser numbers come in T0.4b.
Gates run: check PASS (34 unit tests).
Next: T0.4b.

### 2026-11-17 T0.3b Spike runner
Changed: scripts/spike.ts (`npm run spike`), scripts/lib/{spike-runner,proc-memory}.ts (+ 11 unit tests), vite.spike.config.ts, spike/crash-detection.spec.ts, docs/spike-raw.json (raw results of the full run).
How it works: generates missing files, builds and serves the bench page, one fresh Chromium per run, peak renderer memory from /proc VmHWM via CDP renderer pids, crash/error/timeout recorded as a status, files and build output deleted in a `finally`, exit 1 only when no run finished.
Full run, 9 runs, exit 0, about 5.5 min. SheetJS whole-sheet: xlsx 100k ok (16.9 s, 2.2 GB); xlsx 500k and 1M error "no sheet" after 11 s and 21 s (SheetJS returns no sheet, no exception, probably past the string limit; cause not yet confirmed); csv 100k ok (12.0 s, 2.0 GB); csv 500k ok (70.5 s, 9.3 GB); csv 1M ok (148 s, 10.2 GB). DuckDB read_csv: 100k 0.75 s / 379 MB; 500k 2.7 s / 672 MB; 1M 4.6 s / 1.1 GB. Group-by query 32 to 109 ms. No tab crash happened in the full run.
Gates run: check PASS (22 unit tests); spike PASS (exit 0, 9 runs); spike:smoke crash-detection spec PASS; forced `--timeout-min=0.01` run recorded 3 timeouts, cleaned up, exit 1 as designed.
Next: T0.4 (xlsx strategies for the large files; whole-workbook fails at 500k and 1M).
